Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. Um, well, I guess I'm here because... well, it's a little embarrassing to admit at my age, but I just have a hard time... just sitting. 

I hope I'm explaining this right, but it feels almost like I've got a motor humming under my skin whenever I'm supposed to sit still. Like, if I'm in a staff meeting at work—I'm a building cleaner, so I'm used to being on my feet—or during, you know, church event briefings. I try to be polite, I really do, but I just end up shifting around in my chair a lot, or standing up in the back of the room, or just finding some excuse to go check on something so I can move around. 

It's not that I'm trying to be disrespectful or disruptive, I promise. Does that make sense? Is that a helpful place to start?

Well... I suppose watching TV or reading can be a little tricky sometimes. I really try my best to just sit back and relax, I honestly do. 

But even when I'm doing things that are supposed to be peaceful... like going fishing with my wife, or sitting through a game of bingo... It just gets hard for me to be completely quiet and enjoy it the way other folks do. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound like a nuisance to be around. Oh, goodness, no. I really don't think I do that. I mean... I try very hard to be careful and do things the right way. 

I'd hate to make a mistake that causes trouble for anybody, you know? Well, um... I suppose it has been going on for quite a while, yes. I really was a good boy, I promise, and I always wanted to make my parents and teachers proud. But... I guess I did have a bit of a hard time back in grade school. 

I remember getting spoken to—well, getting in trouble, honestly—for popping out of my seat when I wasn't supposed to. And sometimes I'd accidentally talk over the teacher, or I'd rush ahead on my assignments before she even finished giving us the directions. I never meant any disrespect by it, I really didn't! I just got so eager and ahead of myself, you know? My mother, bless her heart, she used to say I acted like I was wound too tight, even as a little kid. 

I really hope that doesn't make me sound like I was a bad child. I just had a lot of nervous energy, I think. Is that the kind of thing you were looking for, doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.

Well, um... I guess... maybe a little bit? I mean, I really do try to just settle down and enjoy things, especially when I'm with my wife. She's so good to me, and I always want us to have a nice time together.

I just had a lot of nervous energy, I think. Is that the kind of thing you were looking for, doctor? She's so good to me, and I always want us to have a nice time together. \n\nBut sometimes, even when we're doing something we both like, say, if we go fishing on a Sunday, or if we go down to the community center for bingo... I try to just sit quietly, I really do. But I'll catch myself, you know, tapping my foot, or maybe I start organizing my tackle box over and over, or fiddling with the bingo dauber. And sometimes I don't realize I'm talking a bit loud until she gives me a little look. \n\nI don't mean to ruin the peace and quiet! I just... it's like my hands or my mouth just want to keep busy, even when I'm trying my hardest to just relax and be still for her sake. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a nuisance to be around. I really try to be a good companion, doctor, I promise. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I really do try to get along with everyone, and I take a lot of pride in my work. I've been cleaning buildings for a long time, and keeping things tidy is something I care a lot about. I always want to do a good job for the people who work there.

But... I guess lately, there have been a few little hiccups. Nothing terrible, I promise! Just... well, sometimes when we're turning over a room or figuring out the supply closet, I might get a little too eager to just jump in and get it done. And maybe I don't always catch every little detail the supervisor says before I start moving. It's just that I want to be helpful and fast, you know? 

But I think it might be causing a little bit of, well, friction with some of the other folks on the crew. There have been a few complaints, which breaks my heart because I'd never want to upset anyone. My supervisor even gently suggested I look into the employee assistance program. I know he just wants what's best for me, and I want to be the best worker I can be. I really hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad employee, doctor. I'm trying very hard to fix it. Is that the sort of thing you were asking about?

My mind doesn't really wander off or anything like that. \n\nI'm paying attention, I just... I just like to be on my feet while I do it. Does that answer your question okay? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Anxious? Oh, gosh, no. No, I wouldn't say that at all. \n\nI mean, I'm not worried about anything or feeling nervous when I have to sit. I'm actually a pretty easygoing guy, I think. It's really just a physical feeling, you know? Just my body wanting to be busy. I definitely don't feel any anxiety or anything like that. I hope that's okay to say? I just want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture of what's going on, and I wouldn't want you to think I'm a nervous wreck or anything.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble sitting still when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Well, gosh... I suppose I was a bit energetic back then. I really don't want to make it sound like I was a bad kid or disrespectful, because I always respected my teachers! But, um, I do remember getting into a little bit of trouble in grade school. \n\nI'd sort of... pop out of my seat without thinking, or maybe talk over the teacher sometimes by accident. I'd just get so eager to start things that I'd rush ahead before they even finished giving the directions. My mother, bless her heart, she used to say I acted like I was \"wound too tight\" even as a kid. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound like I was a menace or anything? I was a good boy, I just had a lot of pep, I guess. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sitting still when you're watching TV or reading a book?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose watching TV or reading can be a little tricky sometimes. I really try my best to just sit back and relax, I honestly do. \n\nBut even when I'm doing things that are supposed to be peaceful... like going fishing with my wife, or sitting through a game of bingo... I'll catch myself tapping, or maybe talking a little too loud. I always end up messing with my tackle box or fiddling with my pencil instead of just sitting still. It just gets hard for me to be completely quiet and enjoy it the way other folks do. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound like a nuisance to be around. My wife is very sweet about it, and I'd hate for anyone to think I'm doing it on purpose to disrupt things. Is that the sort of thing you mean, doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really don't think I do that. I mean... I try very hard to be careful and do things the right way. \n\nI'd hate to make a mistake that causes trouble for anybody, you know? Like at my job—I clean buildings for a living—and you really have to follow the proper steps with the supplies and everything so it's all safe and tidy. So I always try to think about what I'm doing before I do it. I mean, I know I mentioned getting a little over-eager and maybe talking a bit fast sometimes, but as far as actually doing things without thinking them through... no, I wouldn't say I'm reckless. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I just really wouldn't want you to think I'm irresponsible or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have more trouble sitting still when you're in a boring situation versus an interesting one?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I'm not entirely sure I've noticed a big difference like that. I mean, I suppose everyone gets a little restless when things are dull, right? \n\nBut I really do try my best to pay attention, even if something isn't the most exciting thing in the world. Like when we have staff meetings at work... I know they're important, so I make a real effort to sit quietly and listen to the supervisor. I might shift around a little bit, but I don't think it's any worse than when I'm doing something fun. \n\nI just wouldn't want to seem disrespectful if someone is talking, you know? I always try to be polite, no matter what the situation is. Does that answer your question, doctor?\n\nDoctor: Did your trouble sitting still start when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose it has been going on for quite a while, yes. I really was a good boy, I promise, and I always wanted to make my parents and teachers proud. But... I guess I did have a bit of a hard time back in grade school. \n\nI remember getting spoken to—well, getting in trouble, honestly—for popping out of my seat when I wasn't supposed to. And sometimes I'd accidentally talk over the teacher, or I'd rush ahead on my assignments before she even finished giving us the directions. I never meant any disrespect by it, I really didn't! I just got so eager and ahead of myself, you know? My mother, bless her heart, she used to say I acted like I was wound too tight, even as a little kid. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound like I was a bad child. I just had a lot of nervous energy, I think. Is that the kind of thing you were looking for, doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would have trouble sitting still even if you were doing something you really enjoyed?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess... maybe a little bit? I mean, I really do try to just settle down and enjoy things, especially when I'm with my wife. She's so good to me, and I always want us to have a nice time together. \n\nBut sometimes, even when we're doing something we both like, say, if we go fishing on a Sunday, or if we go down to the community center for bingo... People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, gosh... that’s a really good question, doctor. I want to give you the best answer I can, but... well, um, I’m honestly not quite sure. 

I really do try my absolute hardest to pay attention to everything, especially when people are explaining things to me. I'd hate for anyone to think I'm ignoring them or that I don't care, because I really do. But I've also always been told I have a lot of... well, enthusiasm, I guess you could say. I like to stay busy and keep moving, which is usually a good thing for my line of work! 

So maybe it's just that I have a little extra energy? I just haven't really been able to figure out if it's my mind slipping away that makes me fidget, or if it's just my body wanting to jump up and get to the next task. 

I'm so sorry I don't have a clearer answer for you on that one. I hope that's alright? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.

My mind doesn't really wander off or anything like that. \n\nI'm paying attention, I just... I just like to be on my feet while I do it. Does that answer your question okay? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Anxious? Oh, gosh, no. No, I wouldn't say that at all. \n\nI mean, I'm not worried about anything or feeling nervous when I have to sit. I'm actually a pretty easygoing guy, I think. I just want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture of what's going on, and I wouldn't want you to think I'm a nervous wreck or anything.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble sitting still when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Well, gosh... I'd just get so eager to start things that I'd rush ahead before they even finished giving the directions. My mother, bless her heart, she used to say I acted like I was \"wound too tight\" even as a kid. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound like I was a menace or anything? I was a good boy, I just had a lot of pep, I guess. I'll catch myself tapping, or maybe talking a little too loud. I always end up messing with my tackle box or fiddling with my pencil instead of just sitting still. It just gets hard for me to be completely quiet and enjoy it the way other folks do. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound like a nuisance to be around. My wife is very sweet about it, and I'd hate for anyone to think I'm doing it on purpose to disrupt things. Is that the sort of thing you mean, doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really don't think I do that. I mean... Like at my job—I clean buildings for a living—and you really have to follow the proper steps with the supplies and everything so it's all safe and tidy. So I always try to think about what I'm doing before I do it. I might shift around a little bit, but I don't think it's any worse than when I'm doing something fun. \n\nI just wouldn't want to seem disrespectful if someone is talking, you know? She's so good to me, and I always want us to have a nice time together. \n\nBut sometimes, even when we're doing something we both like, say, if we go fishing on a Sunday, or if we go down to the community center for bingo... Well, um... I'd really like to say yes to that. It just sounds like the right answer, doesn't it? That if you're really fascinated by something, you'd just naturally sit perfectly still and give it all your attention.

I'll catch myself tapping, or maybe talking a little too loud. I always end up messing with my tackle box or fiddling with my pencil instead of just sitting still. It just gets hard for me to be completely quiet and enjoy it the way other folks do. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound like a nuisance to be around. My wife is very sweet about it, and I'd hate for anyone to think I'm doing it on purpose to disrupt things. Is that the sort of thing you mean, doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really don't think I do that. I mean... I try very hard to be careful and do things the right way. \n\nI'd hate to make a mistake that causes trouble for anybody, you know? Like at my job—I clean buildings for a living—and you really have to follow the proper steps with the supplies and everything so it's all safe and tidy. So I always try to think about what I'm doing before I do it. I mean, I know I mentioned getting a little over-eager and maybe talking a bit fast sometimes, but as far as actually doing things without thinking them through... no, I wouldn't say I'm reckless. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I just really wouldn't want you to think I'm irresponsible or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have more trouble sitting still when you're in a boring situation versus an interesting one?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I'm not entirely sure I've noticed a big difference like that. I mean, I suppose everyone gets a little restless when things are dull, right? \n\nBut I really do try my best to pay attention, even if something isn't the most exciting thing in the world. Like when we have staff meetings at work... I know they're important, so I make a real effort to sit quietly and listen to the supervisor. I might shift around a little bit, but I don't think it's any worse than when I'm doing something fun. \n\nI just wouldn't want to seem disrespectful if someone is talking, you know? I always try to be polite, no matter what the situation is. Does that answer your question, doctor?\n\nDoctor: Did your trouble sitting still start when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose it has been going on for quite a while, yes. I really was a good boy, I promise, and I always wanted to make my parents and teachers proud. But... I guess I did have a bit of a hard time back in grade school. \n\nI remember getting spoken to—well, getting in trouble, honestly—for popping out of my seat when I wasn't supposed to. And sometimes I'd accidentally talk over the teacher, or I'd rush ahead on my assignments before she even finished giving us the directions. I never meant any disrespect by it, I really didn't! I just got so eager and ahead of myself, you know? My mother, bless her heart, she used to say I acted like I was wound too tight, even as a little kid. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound like I was a bad child. I just had a lot of nervous energy, I think. Is that the kind of thing you were looking for, doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would have trouble sitting still even if you were doing something you really enjoyed?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess... maybe a little bit? I mean, I really do try to just settle down and enjoy things, especially when I'm with my wife. She's so good to me, and I always want us to have a nice time together. \n\nBut sometimes, even when we're doing something we both like, say, if we go fishing on a Sunday, or if we go down to the community center for bingo... I try to just sit quietly, I really do. But I'll catch myself, you know, tapping my foot, or maybe I start organizing my tackle box over and over, or fiddling with the bingo dauber. And sometimes I don't realize I'm talking a bit loud until she gives me a little look. \n\nI don't mean to ruin the peace and quiet! I just... it's like my hands or my mouth just want to keep busy, even when I'm trying my hardest to just relax and be still for her sake. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a nuisance to be around. I really try to be a good companion, doctor, I promise. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your trouble sitting still is affecting your relationships or work performance?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really do try to get along with everyone, and I take a lot of pride in my work. I've been cleaning buildings for a long time, and keeping things tidy is something I care a lot about. I always want to do a good job for the people who work there.\n\nBut... I guess lately, there have been a few little hiccups. Nothing terrible, I promise! Just... well, sometimes when we're turning over a room or figuring out the supply closet, I might get a little too eager to just jump in and get it done. And maybe I don't always catch every little detail the supervisor says before I start moving. It's just that I want to be helpful and fast, you know? \n\nBut I think it might be causing a little bit of, well, friction with some of the other folks on the crew. There have been a few complaints, which breaks my heart because I'd never want to upset anyone. My supervisor even gently suggested I look into the employee assistance program. I know he just wants what's best for me, and I want to be the best worker I can be. I really hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad employee, doctor. I'm trying very hard to fix it. Is that the sort of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be able to sit still if you were doing an activity that required your full attention?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. I always try my absolute hardest to focus when something is important. I really do. I would certainly try my absolute best! So... I honestly don't know for sure if I could completely stop the fidgeting if I had to just sit there for a very long stretch. I'd really like to think I could control it if I put my mind to it, you know? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, goodness... that's another really interesting thought. You always have such good questions, doctor. 

I suppose... well, I do get a lot of physical activity at work, sweeping and mopping and moving furniture around. I'm on my feet for most of my shift. And when I finally sit down in the breakroom for lunch... I think I'm still bouncing my knee or drumming my fingers on the table. 

But maybe I just didn't work off *enough* energy? Or maybe it's just a habit I've picked up over the years and I don't even realize I'm doing it. I'd really like to think that if I ran a mile or something before sitting down, I'd be perfectly still. I just wouldn't want to promise you that and be wrong.

I'd just get so eager to start things that I'd rush ahead before they even finished giving the directions. My mother, bless her heart, she used to say I acted like I was \"wound too tight\" even as a kid. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound like I was a menace or anything? I was a good boy, I just had a lot of pep, I guess. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sitting still when you're watching TV or reading a book?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose watching TV or reading can be a little tricky sometimes. I really try my best to just sit back and relax, I honestly do. \n\nBut even when I'm doing things that are supposed to be peaceful... like going fishing with my wife, or sitting through a game of bingo... I'll catch myself tapping, or maybe talking a little too loud. I always end up messing with my tackle box or fiddling with my pencil instead of just sitting still. It just gets hard for me to be completely quiet and enjoy it the way other folks do. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound like a nuisance to be around. My wife is very sweet about it, and I'd hate for anyone to think I'm doing it on purpose to disrupt things. It's really just a physical feeling, you know? Just my body wanting to be busy. I definitely don't feel any anxiety or anything like that. I hope that's okay to say? I just want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture of what's going on, and I wouldn't want you to think I'm a nervous wreck or anything.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble sitting still when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Well, gosh... I suppose I was a bit energetic back then. I really don't want to make it sound like I was a bad kid or disrespectful, because I always respected my teachers! But, um, I do remember getting into a little bit of trouble in grade school. \n\nI'd sort of... pop out of my seat without thinking, or maybe talk over the teacher sometimes by accident. I'd just get so eager to start things that I'd rush ahead before they even finished giving the directions. My mother, bless her heart, she used to say I acted like I was \"wound too tight\" even as a kid. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound like I was a menace or anything? I was a good boy, I just had a lot of pep, I guess. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sitting still when you're watching TV or reading a book?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose watching TV or reading can be a little tricky sometimes. I really try my best to just sit back and relax, I honestly do. \n\nBut even when I'm doing things that are supposed to be peaceful... like going fishing with my wife, or sitting through a game of bingo... I'll catch myself tapping, or maybe talking a little too loud. I always end up messing with my tackle box or fiddling with my pencil instead of just sitting still. It just gets hard for me to be completely quiet and enjoy it the way other folks do. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound like a nuisance to be around. My wife is very sweet about it, and I'd hate for anyone to think I'm doing it on purpose to disrupt things. Is that the sort of thing you mean, doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really don't think I do that. I mean... I try very hard to be careful and do things the right way. \n\nI'd hate to make a mistake that causes trouble for anybody, you know? Like at my job—I clean buildings for a living—and you really have to follow the proper steps with the supplies and everything so it's all safe and tidy. So I always try to think about what I'm doing before I do it. I mean, I know I mentioned getting a little over-eager and maybe talking a bit fast sometimes, but as far as actually doing things without thinking them through... no, I wouldn't say I'm reckless. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I just really wouldn't want you to think I'm irresponsible or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have more trouble sitting still when you're in a boring situation versus an interesting one?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I'm not entirely sure I've noticed a big difference like that. I mean, I suppose everyone gets a little restless when things are dull, right? \n\nBut I really do try my best to pay attention, even if something isn't the most exciting thing in the world. Like when we have staff meetings at work... I know they're important, so I make a real effort to sit quietly and listen to the supervisor. I might shift around a little bit, but I don't think it's any worse than when I'm doing something fun. \n\nI just wouldn't want to seem disrespectful if someone is talking, you know? I always try to be polite, no matter what the situation is. Does that answer your question, doctor?\n\nDoctor: Did your trouble sitting still start when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose it has been going on for quite a while, yes. I really was a good boy, I promise, and I always wanted to make my parents and teachers proud. But... I guess I did have a bit of a hard time back in grade school. \n\nI remember getting spoken to—well, getting in trouble, honestly—for popping out of my seat when I wasn't supposed to. And sometimes I'd accidentally talk over the teacher, or I'd rush ahead on my assignments before she even finished giving us the directions. I never meant any disrespect by it, I really didn't! I just got so eager and ahead of myself, you know? My mother, bless her heart, she used to say I acted like I was wound too tight, even as a little kid. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound like I was a bad child. I just had a lot of nervous energy, I think. Is that the kind of thing you were looking for, doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would have trouble sitting still even if you were doing something you really enjoyed?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess... maybe a little bit? I mean, I really do try to just settle down and enjoy things, especially when I'm with my wife. She's so good to me, and I always want us to have a nice time together. \n\nBut sometimes, even when we're doing something we both like, say, if we go fishing on a Sunday, or if we go down to the community center for bingo... I try to just sit quietly, I really do. But I'll catch myself, you know, tapping my foot, or maybe I start organizing my tackle box over and over, or fiddling with the bingo dauber. And sometimes I don't realize I'm talking a bit loud until she gives me a little look. \n\nI don't mean to ruin the peace and quiet! I just... it's like my hands or my mouth just want to keep busy, even when I'm trying my hardest to just relax and be still for her sake. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a nuisance to be around. I really try to be a good companion, doctor, I promise. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your trouble sitting still is affecting your relationships or work performance?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really do try to get along with everyone, and I take a lot of pride in my work. I've been cleaning buildings for a long time, and keeping things tidy is something I care a lot about. I always want to do a good job for the people who work there.\n\nBut... I guess lately, there have been a few little hiccups. Nothing terrible, I promise! Just... well, sometimes when we're turning over a room or figuring out the supply closet, I might get a little too eager to just jump in and get it done. And maybe I don't always catch every little detail the supervisor says before I start moving. It's just that I want to be helpful and fast, you know? \n\nBut I think it might be causing a little bit of, well, friction with some of the other folks on the crew. There have been a few complaints, which breaks my heart because I'd never want to upset anyone. My supervisor even gently suggested I look into the employee assistance program. I know he just wants what's best for me, and I want to be the best worker I can be. I really hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad employee, doctor. I'm trying very hard to fix it. Is that the sort of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be able to sit still if you were doing an activity that required your full attention?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. I always try my absolute hardest to focus when something is important. I really do. If a task needs my full attention, I want to do it right and give it my all, you know? \n\nBut... gosh, I can't really say for certain if I stay perfectly still during those times. I mean, I'd like to think I do! I certainly wouldn't want to be a distraction or mess anything up when it really matters. I just... I guess I haven't really noticed if I'm still tapping my foot or shifting around when I'm really locked into something like that. \n\nI hope it's alright that I don't have a better answer for you. I just haven't kept track of it. Am I making sense, doctor? I just want to make sure I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could sit still if you were doing something that really required you to focus, like solving a puzzle?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really want to say yes, because I do try to be very focused when I need to be. If I'm doing a puzzle, I really want to find the right pieces and put it all together nicely. \n\nBut... I guess I'm not really sure if my body is perfectly still while I'm doing it. I might still be humming a little tune or shifting my weight around without even realizing it. I'd hate to think I couldn't sit still for something like that, but I just haven't really paid close attention to my movements when I'm that deep into a task. I hope that's an okay answer... I'm trying to be as honest as I can, doctor.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your trouble sitting still is more because you have trouble paying attention or because you just have a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... that’s a really good question, doctor. I want to give you the best answer I can, but... well, um, I’m honestly not quite sure. \n\nI really do try my absolute hardest to pay attention to everything, especially when people are explaining things to me. I'd hate for anyone to think I'm ignoring them or that I don't care, because I really do. But I've also always been told I have a lot of... well, enthusiasm, I guess you could say. I like to stay busy and keep moving, which is usually a good thing for my line of work! \n\nSo maybe it's just that I have a little extra energy? I just haven't really been able to figure out if it's my mind slipping away that makes me fidget, or if it's just my body wanting to jump up and get to the next task. \n\nI'm so sorry I don't have a clearer answer for you on that one. I hope that's alright? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be able to control your fidgeting if you were given a task that required you to focus your attention for a long period of time?\n\nPatient: Well, gosh... a long period of time. I would certainly try my absolute best! I always want to do a good job and be respectful of what I'm supposed to be doing. \n\nBut since I'm usually on my feet moving around the building for work, I just haven't had to sit still for hours on end in quite a while. So... I honestly don't know for sure if I could completely stop the fidgeting if I had to just sit there for a very long stretch. I'd really like to think I could control it if I put my mind to it, you know? I just haven't tested it out lately to know for a fact. \n\nI'm so sorry if I'm sounding like a broken record today with all these \"I don't knows.\" I really am trying to give you good answers. Is this still helpful for you, doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would fidget less if you were doing a task that you found extremely interesting?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I'd really like to say yes to that. It just sounds like the right answer, doesn't it? That if you're really fascinated by something, you'd just naturally sit perfectly still and give it all your attention. \n\nBut, to be completely honest with you... I'm just not sure. Even when I'm doing something I really enjoy, like maybe tinkering with a broken floor buffer at work or watching a really good history program at home, I might still be tapping my heel or fiddling with a pen. I'm usually just so caught up in the task that I don't notice what my hands and feet are up to. I've just never really thought to check if I was moving around *less* than usual during those times.\n\nI'd hate to just tell you yes and then have it be the wrong information. I really do want to be as helpful to you as possible. I hope it's okay that I don't quite have a clear answer for that one either... am I still doing alright with these questions, doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fidgeting would stop if you were able to release your excess energy through physical activity before sitting down?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... that's another really interesting thought. You always have such good questions, doctor. \n\nI suppose... well, I do get a lot of physical activity at work, sweeping and mopping and moving furniture around. I'm on my feet for most of my shift. And when I finally sit down in the breakroom for lunch... I think I'm still bouncing my knee or drumming my fingers on the table. \n\nBut maybe I just didn't work off *enough* energy? Or maybe it's just a habit I've picked up over the years and I don't even realize I'm doing it. I'd really like to think that if I ran a mile or something before sitting down, I'd be perfectly still. I just wouldn't want to promise you that and be wrong. \n\nI really haven't kept good track of it, and I'm so sorry I don't have a better answer for you. I feel like I'm not being a very good patient today with all this uncertainty. Am I doing okay? I really want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to help me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fidgeting is more related to having extra energy or having trouble focusing on one thing?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... that's a really good question. Let me think on that for just a second. \n\nI suppose... well, I honestly couldn't say for sure which one it is. I'd hate to guess and give you the wrong impression. I don't really feel like I'm bursting with extra energy or anything—I'm just a regular guy doing my job, you know? And I really do try my absolute hardest to pay attention when I'm supposed to. \n\nBut when my foot starts tapping or I'm jingling my keys in my pocket, I've just never stopped to analyze exactly *why* my body is doing it right in that moment. It just sort of happens, whether I'm trying to figure out a problem with the plumbing or just taking a breather. \n\nI am so sorry, I feel awful that I keep coming up short on these answers for you today. I just want to make sure I'm telling you the exact truth and not just making things up to sound helpful. Is it alright that I don't know how to categorize it like that? I hope I'm not making your job too difficult.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
